c:T69934,<h2 id="charge--generator">Charge = generator</h2>
<p>Quantum mechanics has already shown you one instance: angular momentum <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>L</mi><mi>z</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">L_z</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8333em;vertical-align:-0.15em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">L</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1514em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> generates rotations — sandwich a state with <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>e</mi><mrow><mo>−</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>θ</mi><msub><mi>L</mi><mi>z</mi></msub></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">e^{-i\theta L_z}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8491em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">e</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8491em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">−</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">i</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0278em;">θ</span><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">L</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1645em;"><span style="top:-2.357em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.0714em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.5em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size3 size1 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.143em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> and it comes out rotated. This is universal. Every continuous symmetry has a Noether charge, and once that charge is an operator, commuting with it applies an infinitesimal version of the symmetry:</p>
<p><span class="katex-error" title="ParseError: KaTeX parse error: \tag works only in display equations" style="color:#cc0000"> \delta(\text{anything}) = [\,iG,\;\text{anything}\,]. \tag{1}</span></p>
<p>The charge and the generator are one object. Momentum generates translations; the Lorentz charge generates Lorentz transformations. So "check that <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>p</mi><mi>μ</mi></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">p^\mu</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8588em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">μ</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> generates translations" is not busywork — it verifies that the operator built from the quantum theory still does the geometric job its classical ancestor did.</p>
<p>Why bother for a single point particle? Because the whole question funnels into a Lie-algebra closure test, and the particle passes it trivially. Watching it pass — seeing exactly which commutator carries the danger — is what lets you recognize, in Modules 14 and 16, the moment the string version fails unless spacetime is 26-dimensional. This note is the control experiment.</p>
<p>In applied-math terms, the charges give a representation of the Poincaré Lie algebra on the operator algebra. Verifying brackets like <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><msup><mi>M</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msup><mo separator="true">,</mo><msup><mi>M</mi><mrow><mi>ρ</mi><mi>σ</mi></mrow></msup><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">[M^{\mu\nu},M^{\rho\sigma}]</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">[</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">M</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0637em;">μν</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mpunct">,</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">M</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">ρ</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0359em;">σ</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose">]</span></span></span></span> checks that the alleged symmetry operators satisfy the defining relations of the group; if they don't, the "symmetry" was a fiction.</p>
<p>One convention in (1) needs pinning down. For a translation with parameter <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>ϵ</mi><mi>μ</mi></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon^\mu</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6644em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">ϵ</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">μ</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> the generator is <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>G</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>ϵ</mi><mi>ρ</mi></msub><msup><mi>p</mi><mi>ρ</mi></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G=\epsilon_\rho p^\rho</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">G</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.9505em;vertical-align:-0.2861em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">ϵ</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1514em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">ρ</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2861em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span mtight">ρ</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>. For a Lorentz transformation the parameter <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>ϵ</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon^{\mu\nu}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6644em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">ϵ</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0637em;">μν</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> is antisymmetric, so the generator is <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>G</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mstyle scriptlevel="0" displaystyle="false"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle><msub><mi>ϵ</mi><mrow><mi>ρ</mi><mi>σ</mi></mrow></msub><msup><mi>M</mi><mrow><mi>ρ</mi><mi>σ</mi></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation The factor <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mstyle scriptlevel="0" displaystyle="false"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tfrac12</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.1901em;vertical-align:-0.345em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mopen nulldelimiter"></span><span class="mfrac"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8451em;"><span style="top:-2.655em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span><span style="top:-3.23em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="frac-line" style="border-bottom-width:0.04em;"></span></span><span style="top:-3.394em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">1</span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.345em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose nulldelimiter"></span></span></span></span></span> avoids double-counting the antisymmetric pair.</p>

The commutator returns exactly the shift <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>ϵ</mi><mi>μ</mi></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon^\mu</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6644em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">ϵ</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">μ</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>: the charge is the generator. This used the covariant commutator; in light-cone gauge one component misbehaves, which is the next order of business.</p>
<h3 id="the-light-cone-twist-p--drags-the-gauge-with-it">The light-cone twist: <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>p</mi><mo>−</mo></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">p^-</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.9658em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.7713em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mbin mtight">−</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> drags the gauge with it</h3>
<p>In light-cone gauge the mass shell has already been solved, so <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math Split the generator into light-cone pieces:

The coordinate that should move sits still, and coordinates that should sit still move. What happened?</p>
<p>The resolution is that <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>p</mi><mo>−</mo></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">p^-</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.9658em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.7713em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mbin mtight">−</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> generates a translation <em>and</em> a reparameterization of the worldline. A reparameterization <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>τ</mi><mo>→</mo><mi>τ</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>λ</mi><mo The physical logic: a bare shift <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math Demand that translation plus reparameterization leave <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">x^+</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7713em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">x</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.7713em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mbin mtight">+</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> in its gauge form:

mtight">−</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> and the anomalous motions above come back exactly.</p>
<blockquote>
<p>Fixing a gauge gives the generators extra structure: <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>p</mi><mo>−</mo></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">p^-</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.9658em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.7713em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mbin mtight">−</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> cannot just translate, it must translate and re-glue the gauge, and the extra motion is the receipt for the gauge choice. The string does the same thing on a larger scale — gauge-fixed generators acquire oscillator tails, and Lorentz invariance stops being automatic.</p>
</blockquote>

class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7429em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord mathnormal">i</span></span></span></span> flagged in <a href="/rome-wiki/articles/string-theory/part-iii-quantization/13-quantum-relativistic-particle/canonical-commutators/" class="wiki-link">Canonical commutators</a>. Track that sign; it propagates.</p>
<h2 id="lorentz-charges-and-their-algebra">Lorentz charges and their algebra</h2>
<p>The particle action is Lorentz invariant: under <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>δ</mi><msup><mi>x</mi><mi>μ</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>ϵ</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msup><msub><mi>x</mi><mi>ν</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\delta x^\mu=\epsilon^{\mu\nu}x_\nu</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0379em;">δ</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">x</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">μ</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8144em;vertical-align:-0.15em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">ϵ</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0637em;">μν</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">x</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1514em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0637em;">ν</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> with <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>ϵ</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><msup><mi>ϵ</mi><mrow><mi>ν</mi><mi>μ</mi></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon^{\mu\nu}=-\epsilon^{\nu\mu}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6644em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">ϵ</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0637em;">μν</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7477em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">ϵ</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0637em;">ν</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">μ</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>, Noether gives the charges
<span class="katex-error" title="ParseError: KaTeX parse error: \tag works only in display equations" style="color:#cc0000"> \boxed{\;M^{\mu\nu}=x^\mu p^\nu - x^\nu p^\mu\;}\tag{3}</span>
This is orbital angular momentum, relativistically completed: the spatial <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>M</mi><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>j</mi></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M^{ij}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8247em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">M</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8247em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0572em;">ij</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> are ordinary angular momenta, the mixed <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>M</mi><mrow><mn>0</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M^{0i}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8247em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">M</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8247em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">0</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">i</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> are boosts. It is Hermitian — rearranging <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>x</mi><mi>p</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">xp</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">x</span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span></span></span></span> back to <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>p</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">px</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="mord mathnormal">x</span></span></span></span> produces two c-number pieces that cancel — as any generator of a unitary symmetry must be.</p>
<p>These charges generate Lorentz transformations. The one commutator needed is
<span class="katex-error" title="ParseError: KaTeX parse error: \tag works only in display equations" style="color:#cc0000"> [M^{\mu\nu},x^\rho]=x^\mu[p^\nu,x^\rho]-x^\nu[p^\mu,x^\rho]=i\eta^{\mu\rho}x^\nu-i\eta^{\nu\rho}x^\mu, \tag{4}</span>
which says the generator rotates/boosts <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>x</mi><mi>ρ</mi></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">x^\rho</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6644em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">x</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">ρ</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> into the two coordinates whose labels sit on <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>M</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M^{\mu\nu}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">M</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0637em;">μν</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>. Then
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>δ</mi><msup><mi>x</mi><mi>ρ</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><mo fence="false" stretchy="true" minsize="1.8em" maxsize="1.8em">[</mo><mo>−</mo><mstyle scriptlevel="0" displaystyle="false"><mfrac><mi>i</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle><msub><mi>ϵ</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msub><msup><mi>M</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msup><mo separator="true">,</mo><mtext> </mtext><msup><mi>x</mi><mi>ρ</mi></msup><mo fence="false" stretchy="true" minsize="1.8em" maxsize="1.8em">]</mo><mo>=</mo><mstyle scriptlevel="0" displaystyle="false"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle><msub><mi>ϵ</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msub><mo fence="false" stretchy="true" minsize="1.2em" maxsize="1.2em">(</mo><msup><mi>η</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ρ</mi></mrow></msup><msup><mi>x</mi><mi>ν</mi></msup><mo>−</mo><msup><mi>η</mi><mrow><mi>ν</mi><mi>ρ</mi></mrow></msup><msup><mi>x</mi><mi>μ</mi></msup><mo fence="false" stretchy="true" minsize="1.2em" maxsize="1.2em">)</mo><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>ϵ</mi><mrow><mi>ρ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msup><msub><mi>x</mi><mi>ν</mi></msub><mo separator="true">,</mo><mtext> </mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">✓</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\delta x^\rho=\Big[-\tfrac{i}{2}\epsilon_{\mu\nu}M^{\mu\nu},\,x^\rho\Big]=\tfrac12\epsilon_{\mu\nu}\big(\eta^{\mu\rho}x^\nu-\eta^{\nu\rho}x^\mu\big)=\epsilon^{\rho\nu}x_\nu,\ \checkmark</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0379em;">δ</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">x</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">ρ</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.8em;vertical-align:-0.65em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="delimsizing size2">[</span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">−</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.8em;vertical-align:-0.65em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mopen nulldelimiter"></span><span class="mfrac"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">i</span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" mathnormal">x</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" class="delimsizing size2">]</span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.2em;vertical-align:-0.35em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0359em;">η</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">μ</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mord"><span class="delimsizing size1">)</span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8867em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">ϵ</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">ρ</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0637em;">ν</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">x</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1514em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0637em;">ν</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mpunct">,</span><span class="mspace"> </span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord amsrm">✓</span></span></span></span>
recovering the transformation we started from. The identical computation on momentum gives
<span class="katex-error" title="ParseError: KaTeX parse error: \tag works only in display equations" style="color:#cc0000"> [M^{\mu\nu},p^\rho]=i\eta^{\mu\rho}p^\nu-i\eta^{\nu\rho}p^\mu, \tag{5}</span>
so <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>p</mi><mi>ρ</mi></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">p^\rho</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8588em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">ρ</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> is a Lorentz vector too — the missing ingredient for the algebra.</p>
<h3 id="the-lorentz-algebra--the-payoff">The Lorentz algebra — the payoff</h3>
<p>Now compute the bracket of two Lorentz generators. Expand <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><msup><mi>M</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msup><mo [M^{\mu\nu},M^{\rho\sigma}]
&#x26;=[M^{\mu\nu},x^\rho]\,p^\sigma+x^\rho[M^{\mu\nu},p^\sigma]-[M^{\mu\nu},x^\sigma]\,p^\rho-x^\sigma[M^{\mu\nu},p^\rho].

<p>Each of the four terms produces a piece of the form <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>η</mi><mtext> </mtext><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mi>κ</mi></msup><msup><mi>p</mi><mi>λ</mi></msup><mo>−</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mi>λ</mi></msup><msup><mi>p</mi><mi>κ</mi></msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>=</mo><mi>η</mi><mtext> </mtext><msup><mi>M</mi><mrow><mi>κ</mi><mi>λ</mi></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\eta\,(x^\kappa p^\lambda-x^\lambda p^\kappa)=\eta\,M^{\kappa\lambda}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0991em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0359em;">η</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">x</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">κ</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8491em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">λ</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">−</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0991em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">x</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8491em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">λ</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">κ</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0435em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0359em;">η</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">M</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8491em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">κλ</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>; collecting them gives the closed relation
<span class="katex-error" title="ParseError: KaTeX parse error: \tag works only in display equations" style="color:#cc0000"> \boxed{\;[M^{\mu\nu},M^{\rho\sigma}]=i\eta^{\mu\rho}M^{\nu\sigma}-i\eta^{\nu\rho}M^{\mu\sigma}+i\eta^{\mu\sigma}M^{\rho\nu}-i\eta^{\nu\sigma}M^{\rho\mu}\;}\tag{6}</span></p>
<p>This is the defining relation of the Lorentz Lie algebra, and it is the headline of the whole module:</p>
<blockquote>
<p>Any Lorentz-invariant quantum theory must possess operators <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>M</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M^{\mu\nu}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">M</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0637em;">μν</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> obeying (6); if you cannot build them, the theory is not Lorentz invariant. For the point particle this comes free — <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>M</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M^{\mu\nu}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">M</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0637em;">μν</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> is a simple bilinear and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">m^2</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8141em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">m</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> is just a number. For the light-cone string, closure of one specific bracket, <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><msup><mi>M</mi><mrow><mo>−</mo><mi>I</mi></mrow></msup><mo separator="true">,</mo><msup><mi>M</mi><mrow><mo>−</mo><mi>J</mi></mrow></msup><mo stretchy="false">]</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">[M^{-I},M^{-J}]=0</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0913em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">[</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">M</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8413em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">−</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">I</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mpunct">,</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">M</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8413em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">−</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0962em;">J</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose">]</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">0</span></span></span></span>, fails unless the spacetime dimension is <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>D</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>26</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">D=26</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0278em;">D</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">26</span></span></span></span> and the normal-ordering constant is <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">a=-1</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">a</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord">1</span></span></span></span>. That is where the number 26 is born.</p>
</blockquote>
<p>To read off (6): each <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>η</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\eta</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0359em;">η</span></span></span></span> carries one index from each generator, and antisymmetry in <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mtext> ⁣</mtext><mo>↔</mo><mtext> ⁣</mtext><mi>ν</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mu\!\leftrightarrow\!\nu</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">μ</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:-0.1667em;"></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">↔</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:-0.1667em;"></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0637em;">ν</span></span></span></span> and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>ρ</mi><mtext> ⁣</mtext><mo>↔</mo><mtext> ⁣</mtext><mi>σ</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\rho\!\leftrightarrow\!\sigma</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ρ</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:-0.1667em;"></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">↔</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:-0.1667em;"></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0359em;">σ</span></span></span></span> then fixes all four terms and their signs. Two consequences in light-cone coordinates:
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><msup><mi>M</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mo>−</mo></mrow></msup><mo separator="true">,</mo><msup><mi>M</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mi>I</mi></mrow></msup><mo stretchy="false">]</mo><mo>=</mo><mi>i</mi><msup><mi>M</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mi>I</mi></mrow></msup><mo separator="true">,</mo><mspace width="2em"></mspace><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><msup><mi>M</mi><mrow><mo>−</mo><mi>I</mi></mrow></msup><mo separator="true">,</mo><msup><mi>M</mi><mrow><mo>−</mo><mi>J</mi></mrow></msup><mo stretchy="false">]</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0.</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">[M^{+-},M^{+I}]=iM^{+I},\qquad [M^{-I},M^{-J}]=0.</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0913em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">[</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">M</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.7713em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">+−</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mpunct">,</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">M</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8413em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">+</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">I</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose">]</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0913em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">i</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">M</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8413em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">−</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">I</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mpunct">,</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">M</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8413em;"><span The first uses <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>η</mi><mrow><mo>−</mo><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\eta^{-+}=-1</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.9658em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0359em;">η</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.7713em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">−+</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord">1</span></span></span></span>, the only nonzero <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>η</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\eta</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0359em;">η</span></span></span></span> available. The second is automatic for the covariant charges: the only <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>η</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\eta</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0359em;">η</span></span></span></span> on offer would force an <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>M</mi><mrow><mo>−</mo><mo>−</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M^{--}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7713em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">M</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.7713em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">−−</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>, which vanishes by antisymmetry. It is the light-cone-<em>gauge</em> version of this bracket that bites the string, so we watch the particle pass it.</p>
<h2 id="light-cone-gauge-generators-and-the-closure-test">Light-cone gauge generators and the closure test</h2>
<p>The covariant formula (3) still guides the construction, but now <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math The explicit <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>τ</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tau</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1132em;">τ</span></span></span></span> terms cancel (as they must for conserved charges), leaving the easy pieces

<p>For <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>M</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mo>−</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation mtight"><span class="mord mtight">0</span></span></span><span style="top:-3.1031em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 style="height:0.2663em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.9658em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.7713em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" Gauge fixing has made Hermiticity non-automatic; the fix is to symmetrize:
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>M</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mo>−</mo></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mstyle scriptlevel="0" displaystyle="false"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle><mo fence="false" stretchy="true" minsize="1.2em" maxsize="1.2em">(</mo><msubsup><mi>x</mi><mn>0</mn><mo>−</mo></msubsup><msup><mi>p</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>p</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><msubsup><mi>x</mi><mn>0</mn><mo>−</mo></msubsup><mo fence="false" stretchy="true" minsize="1.2em" maxsize="1.2em">)</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">.</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M^{+-}=-\tfrac12\big(x_0^-p^+ + p^+x_0^-\big).</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7713em;"></span><span class="mord"><span nulldelimiter"></span><span class="mfrac"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8451em;"><span style="top:-2.655em;"><span class="pstrut" This is not cosmetic — a Lorentz generator is an observable, so it must be Hermitian.</p>
<p>The dangerous generator is <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>M</mi><mrow><mo>−</mo><mi>I</mi></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M^{-I}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8413em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">M</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8413em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">−</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">I</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>, the light-cone analog of the string's deadly boost. Symmetrization is again needed, since <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><msubsup><mi>x</mi><mn>0</mn><mi>I</mi></msubsup><mo separator="true">,</mo><msup><mi>p</mi><mo>−</mo></msup><mo stretchy="false">]</mo><mo>=</mo><mi>i</mi><mtext> </mtext><msup><mi>p</mi><mi>I</mi></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">/</mi><msup><mi>p</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo is symmetric in <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>I</mi><mo separator="true">,</mo><mi>J</mi></mrow><annotation class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.59em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>
for the light-cone-gauge point particle.</p>
<blockquote>
<p>The particle passes the Lorentz test. After the correct Hermitian ordering, the reduced light-cone operators — even the awkward <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>M</mi><mrow><mo>−</mo><mi>I</mi></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M^{-I}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8413em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">M</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8413em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">−</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">I</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> — still realize the full Lorentz algebra, with no constraint on the dimension. The string runs this same calculation with <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>M</mi><mrow><mo>−</mo><mi>I</mi></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M^{-I}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8413em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">M</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8413em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">−</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">I</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> now an infinite oscillator sum: the boost no longer annihilates the operator-valued mass, a normal-ordering anomaly appears, and the zero on the right survives only for <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>D</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>26</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">D=26</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0278em;">D</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">26</span></span></span></span>, <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">a=-1</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">a</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord">1</span></span></span></span>. One catastrophic new term separates the two. Remember the shape here and the string result will feel inevitable rather than magical.</p>
</blockquote>
<h2 id="what-to-carry-forward">What to carry forward</h2>
<ul>
<li><strong>Charge = generator:</strong> <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>δ</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mtext>anything</mtext><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>=</mo><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mtext> </mtext><mi>i</mi><mi>G</mi><mo separator="true">,</mo><mtext> </mtext><mtext>anything</mtext><mtext> </mtext><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\delta(\text{anything})=[\,iG,\,\text{anything}\,]</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0379em;">δ</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord text"><span class="mord">anything</span></span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">[</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">i</span><span class="mord mathnormal">G</span><span class="mpunct">,</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord text"><span class="mord">anything</span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mclose">]</span></span></span></span>.</li>
<li><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>p</mi><mi>μ</mi></msup><mo>→</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">p^\mu\to</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8588em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">μ</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">→</span></span></span></span> translations (mutually commuting); <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>M</mi><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mi>ν</mi></mrow></msup><mo>→</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M^{\mu\nu}\to</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">M</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0637em;">μν</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">→</span></span></span></span> Lorentz, closing into (6).</li>
<li>Gauge fixing makes <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>p</mi><mo>−</mo></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">p^-</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.9658em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" mtight">−</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> generate translation + reparameterization, and forces Hermitian symmetrization of <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>M</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mo>−</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">M</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.7713em;"><span mtight">+−</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>M</mi><mrow><mo>−</mo><mi>I</mi></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation <li>The particle's light-cone Lorentz algebra closes unconditionally. The string's <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><msup><mi>M</mi><mrow><mo>−</mo><mi>I</mi></mrow></msup><mo separator="true">,</mo><msup><mi>M</mi><mrow><mo>−</mo><mi>J</mi></mrow></msup><mo class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">0</span></span></span></span> is the same bracket — and it is where <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>D</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>26</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">D=26</annotation></semantics></math></span><span </ul>
<h2 id="open-questions">Open questions</h2>
<ul>
<li>Treating <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msubsup><mi>x</mi><mn>0</mn><mo>−</mo></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mi>i</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><msup><mi>p</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">x_0^-=-i\partial_{p^+}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0778em;vertical-align:-0.2663em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">x</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8115em;"><span style="top:-2.4337em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">0</span></span></span><span style="top:-3.1031em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mbin mtight">−</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2663em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.9805em;vertical-align:-0.2861em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord mathnormal">i</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">p</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.7027em;"><span style="top:-2.786em;margin-right:0.0714em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.5em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size3 size1 mtight"><span class="mbin mtight">+</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2861em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> as a self-adjoint operator on the half-line <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math mtight">+</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">0</span></span></span></span> needs a domain analysis this note skips. The commutators are algebraically correct; rigor belongs with covariant quantization or a dedicated light-cone Hilbert-space note.</li>
<li>In the string, <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>M</mi><mrow><mo>−</mo><mi>I</mi></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M^{-I}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8413em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">M</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8413em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">−</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">I</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> carries oscillator bilinears and its commutator develops the normal-ordering anomaly. Modules 14 and 16 show why the anomaly cancels only at <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>D</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>26</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">D=26</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0278em;">D</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">26</span></span></span></span>, <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">a=-1</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">a</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord">1</span></span></span></span> — see <a href="/rome-wiki/articles/string-theory/part-iii-quantization/14-quantum-open-strings/light-cone-lorentz-anomaly/" class="wiki-link">Light-cone Lorentz anomaly</a> for the sequel this note is the prequel to.</li>
</ul>
<h2 id="exercise">Exercise</h2>
<p>The closure <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo boost vector field annihilates the energy, <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math
